[*] Waasland-Beveren successfully protested their relegation at the Belgium
    sports arbitration court (BAS), on the grounds that the meeting on May 15
    was not legally valid; this was confirmed by a court in Dendermonde on
    Jul 30; on Jul 31 it was decided to extend the league to 18 clubs for the
    2020/21 season: Waasland-Beveren remain at the first level and OH Leuven
    and Beerschot both were promoted.

NB: league suspended due to Covid-19 pandemic; on Apr 2, the board of directors advised
    to abandon the season and declare Club Brugge champions; the final decision on this
    was taken at a general meeting of the clubs on May 15 (earlier, that decision had
    been announced for Apr 15, Apr 24, Apr 27 and May 4).

Promotion Playoff

First Leg [Mar 8]
Beerschot         1-0 OH Leuven         

Second Leg [Aug 2; postponed from Mar 14 after mayor of Leuven forbade the match]
OH Leuven         1-4 Beerschot         

NB: if the second leg would not have been played, Westerlo would have been promoted
    as leaders of the aggregate table;
    however, on Jul 31 it was decided to extend the top level to 18 clubs and both
    Beerschot and OH Leuven were promoted ahead of the second leg;
    on Aug 3, Westerlo started court proceedings demanding promotion.

Playoff III

Table:

 7.KSV Roeselare                     0   0  0  0   0- 0   3  [3]
-----------------------------------------------------------
 8.KSC Lokeren OV                    0   0  0  0   0- 0   0

NB: was to be played over 5 matches, with Roeselare receiving 3 bonus points and
    one more home fixture because of better record in the aggregate table;
    however, the playoff was first postponed due to the Covid-19 pandemic and 
    eventually cancelled as Lokeren were declared bankrupt on Apr 20 and folded.

NB: both Excelsior Virton and Roeselare were denied a professional license for
    the 2020/21 season; their places were taken by the two highest finishers
    (after KMSK Deinze) in the Amateur First Class to have obtained a professional
    license, viz. FC Seraing and Racing White Daring Molenbeek;
    moreover, as eventually no clubs were relegated from the first to the second
    level while two were promoted, two additional places became available at
    the second level, which were given to Lierse Kempenzonen (the only other
    club from the third level to have obtained a professional license) and to
    Club Brugge NXT (the youth academy side of Club Brugge KV).
